## Password Input

A styled and accessible password input component that captures sensitive user credentials with masked text display.

```tsx
import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"
```

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#simple)

The simple API bundles all subcomponents together into a single component.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

export function PasswordInputSimpleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <PasswordInput
      className="w-72"
      clearable
      hint="Optional hint"
      label="Password"
      placeholder="Create password"
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Icons (Simple)](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#icons-simple)

Add a [startIcon](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#startIcon) to the simple `<PasswordInput>` using the prop at the root. Unlike the [`<TextInput>`](/components/text-input), the `endIcon` is not supported because its slot is reserved for the password visibility trigger.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {KeyRound} from "lucide-react"

import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

export function PasswordInputIconsDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <PasswordInput
      className="w-72"
      label="Password"
      placeholder="Create password"
      startIcon={KeyRound}
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#composite)

The composite pattern provides full control over the component's elements by composing individual subcomponents.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

export function PasswordInputCompositeDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <PasswordInput.Root className="w-72">
      <PasswordInput.Label>Password</PasswordInput.Label>
      <PasswordInput.InputGroup>
        <PasswordInput.Input placeholder="Placeholder text" />
        <PasswordInput.VisibilityTrigger />
      </PasswordInput.InputGroup>
      <PasswordInput.Hint>Optional hint</PasswordInput.Hint>
    </PasswordInput.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Controlled Value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#controlled-value)

Set the initial value using the [defaultValue](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#defaultValue) prop, or use [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#value) and [onValueChange](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#onValueChange) to control the value manually. These props follow our [controlled state](/patterns/controlled-state) pattern.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useState} from "react"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

export function PasswordInputControlledValueDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<string>("passw0rd")

  return (
    <div className="flex items-end gap-4">
      <PasswordInput
        className="w-72"
        label="Password"
        onValueChange={(updatedValue) => {
          console.debug("Value changed:", updatedValue)
          setValue(updatedValue)
        }}
        placeholder="Enter your password"
        value={value}
      />

      <Button emphasis="primary" onClick={() => setValue("")} variant="outline">
        Reset
      </Button>
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Controlled Visibility](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#controlled-visibility)

Set the initial visibility using the [defaultVisible](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#defaultVisible) prop, or use [visible](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#visible) and [onVisibleChange](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#onVisibleChange) to control the visibility manually. These props follow our [controlled state](/patterns/controlled-state) pattern.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useState} from "react"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

export function PasswordInputControlledVisibilityDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [visible, setVisible] = useState<boolean>(false)

  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-4">
      <PasswordInput
        className="w-72"
        defaultValue="passw0rd"
        label="Password"
        onVisibleChange={setVisible}
        placeholder="Enter your password"
        visible={visible}
      />

      <Button
        emphasis="primary"
        onClick={() => setVisible(!visible)}
        variant="outline"
      >
        {visible ? "Hide Password" : "Show Password"}
      </Button>
    </div>
  )
}

```

### [Forms](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#forms)

#### [React Hook Form](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#react-hook-form)

```tsx
import {arktypeResolver} from "@hookform/resolvers/arktype"
import {type} from "arktype"
import {Controller, useForm} from "react-hook-form"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

interface PasswordFormData {
  confirmPassword: string
  password: string
}

// Arktype schema
const passwordSchema = type({
  confirmPassword: "string",
  password: "string",
}).narrow(({confirmPassword, password}, ctx) => {
  let passwordError: string | undefined
  if (!password || password.trim().length === 0) {
    passwordError = "Please enter your password"
  } else if (password.length < 8) {
    passwordError = "Must be at least 8 characters long"
  } else if (!/(?=.*[a-z])/.test(password)) {
    passwordError = "Must contain at least one lowercase letter"
  } else if (!/(?=.*[A-Z])/.test(password)) {
    passwordError = "Must contain at least one uppercase letter"
  } else if (!/(?=.*\d)/.test(password)) {
    passwordError = "Must contain at least one number"
  } else if (!/(?=.*[@$!%*?&])/.test(password)) {
    passwordError = "Must contain at least one special character (@$!%*?&)"
  }

  let valid = true
  if (passwordError) {
    valid = false
    ctx.reject({message: passwordError, path: ["password"]})
  }
  if (!confirmPassword) {
    valid = false
    ctx.reject({
      message: "Please confirm your password",
      path: ["confirmPassword"],
    })
  } else if (password !== confirmPassword) {
    valid = false
    ctx.reject({
      // don't display the password in the error message
      actual: "",
      message: "Passwords do not match",
      path: ["confirmPassword"],
    })
  }
  return valid
})

export function PasswordInputReactHookFormDemo() {
  const {
    control,
    formState: {errors, isSubmitting, submitCount, touchedFields},
    handleSubmit,
  } = useForm<PasswordFormData>({
    defaultValues: {
      confirmPassword: "",
      password: "",
    },
    mode: "onChange",
    resolver: arktypeResolver(passwordSchema),
  })

  const onSubmit = (data: PasswordFormData) => {
    console.log("Form submitted:", data)
  }

  const shouldShowError = (fieldName: keyof PasswordFormData) => {
    return (submitCount > 0 || touchedFields[fieldName]) && errors[fieldName]
  }

  return (
    <form
      className="mx-auto flex w-full max-w-xs flex-col gap-3"
      onSubmit={(e) => {
        void handleSubmit(onSubmit)(e)
      }}
    >
      <div className="grid grid-cols-1 gap-4">
        <div>
          <Controller
            control={control}
            name="password"
            render={({field: {onChange, ...fieldProps}}) => {
              return (
                <PasswordInput
                  errorText={errors.password?.message}
                  hint={
                    shouldShowError("password")
                      ? null
                      : "must be 8+ characters with at least 1 number, lowercase, uppercase, and special character."
                  }
                  invalid={!!shouldShowError("password")}
                  label="Password"
                  onValueChange={onChange}
                  placeholder="Create password"
                  {...fieldProps}
                />
              )
            }}
          />
        </div>

        <div>
          <Controller
            control={control}
            name="confirmPassword"
            render={({field: {onChange, ...fieldProps}}) => {
              return (
                <PasswordInput
                  errorText={errors.confirmPassword?.message}
                  invalid={!!errors.confirmPassword}
                  label="Confirm password"
                  onValueChange={onChange}
                  placeholder="Confirm password"
                  {...fieldProps}
                />
              )
            }}
          />
        </div>
      </div>

      <div className="mt-2 flex w-full justify-end">
        <Button
          disabled={isSubmitting}
          emphasis="primary"
          type="submit"
          variant="fill"
        >
          Submit
        </Button>
      </div>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [TanStack Form](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#tanstack-form)

```tsx
import {useForm} from "@tanstack/react-form"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {PasswordInput} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/password-input"

interface PasswordFormData {
  confirmPassword: string
  password: string
}

// Password validation utility
const validatePassword = (password: string): string | undefined => {
  if (!password || password.trim().length === 0) {
    return "Please enter your password"
  }
  if (password.length < 8) {
    return "Must be at least 8 characters long"
  }
  if (!/(?=.*[a-z])/.test(password)) {
    return "Must contain at least one lowercase letter"
  }
  if (!/(?=.*[A-Z])/.test(password)) {
    return "Must contain at least one uppercase letter"
  }
  if (!/(?=.*\d)/.test(password)) {
    return "Must contain at least one number"
  }
  if (!/(?=.*[@$!%*?&])/.test(password)) {
    return "Must contain at least one special character (@$!%*?&)"
  }
  return undefined
}

export function PasswordInputTanstackFormDemo() {
  const form = useForm({
    canSubmitWhenInvalid: true,
    defaultValues: {
      confirmPassword: "",
      password: "",
    } satisfies PasswordFormData,
    onSubmit: ({value}) => {
      console.log("Form submitted:", value)
    },
  })

  return (
    <form
      className="mx-auto flex w-full max-w-xs flex-col gap-3"
      onSubmit={(e) => {
        e.preventDefault()
        form.handleSubmit()
      }}
    >
      <div className="grid grid-cols-1 gap-4">
        <form.Field
          name="password"
          validators={{
            onBlur: ({value}) => validatePassword(value),
            onChange: ({fieldApi, value}) => {
              const error = validatePassword(value)
              if (!error) {
                fieldApi.setErrorMap({onBlur: undefined})
              }
              return error
            },
          }}
        >
          {(field) => {
            // only show the error if the user has attempted to submit the form, or
            // this field has been blurred. This prevents the error from showing
            // immediately as the user types.
            const isInvalid =
              (form.state.submissionAttempts > 0 ||
                field.getMeta().isBlurred) &&
              !!field.state.meta.errors?.at(0)
            return (
              <PasswordInput
                errorText={field.state.meta.errors?.at(0)}
                hint={
                  isInvalid
                    ? null
                    : "must be 8+ characters with at least 1 number, lowercase, uppercase, and special character."
                }
                invalid={isInvalid}
                label="Password"
                name={field.name}
                onBlur={field.handleBlur}
                onValueChange={field.handleChange}
                placeholder="Create password"
                value={field.state.value}
              />
            )
          }}
        </form.Field>

        <form.Field
          name="confirmPassword"
          validators={{
            onBlur: ({fieldApi, value}) => {
              if (!value || value.trim().length === 0) {
                return "Please confirm your password"
              }

              const passwordValue = fieldApi.form.getFieldValue("password")
              if (value !== passwordValue) {
                return "Passwords do not match"
              }

              return undefined
            },
            onChange: ({fieldApi, value}) => {
              if (!value || value.trim().length === 0) {
                return "Please confirm your password"
              }

              const passwordValue = fieldApi.form.getFieldValue("password")
              if (value !== passwordValue) {
                return "Passwords do not match"
              }

              // clear onBlur error
              fieldApi.setErrorMap({onBlur: undefined})
              return undefined
            },
          }}
        >
          {(field) => (
            <PasswordInput
              errorText={field.state.meta.errors?.at(0)}
              invalid={field.state.meta.errors.length > 0}
              label="Confirm password"
              name={field.name}
              onBlur={field.handleBlur}
              onValueChange={field.handleChange}
              placeholder="Confirm password"
              value={field.state.value}
            />
          )}
        </form.Field>
      </div>

      <div className="mt-2 flex w-full justify-end">
        <Button
          disabled={form.state.isSubmitting}
          emphasis="primary"
          type="submit"
          variant="fill"
        >
          Submit
        </Button>
      </div>
    </form>
  )
}

```

### [API](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#api)

#### [\<PasswordInput>](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#password-input)

The `PasswordInput` extends the [PasswordInput.Root](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/password-input#password-input-root) with the following props:

```json
[
  {
    "name": "autoComplete",
    "type": "'current-password' | 'new-password'",
    "defaultValue": "\"current-password\"",
    "description": "The [autocomplete](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Attributes/autocomplete) attribute for the password input."
  },
  {
    "name": "children",
    "type": "never",
    "description": "The simple PasswordInput doesn't support children."
  },
  {
    "name": "clearable",
    "type": "boolean",
    "defaultValue": "false",
    "description": "When `true`, renders a clear button that resets the input value on click. The button only appears when the input has a value."
  },
  {
    "name": "defaultValue",
    "type": "string",
```

## Popover

Popovers are containers used to display transient content such as options and actions.

```typescript
import {Popover} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/popover"
```

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#simple)

Basic popover using the simple API with a label and child content.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Popover} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/popover"

export function PopoverSimpleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Popover
      label="Label"
      trigger={<Button emphasis="primary">Show Popover</Button>}
    >
      Popover content
    </Popover>
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#composite)

Build with the composite API for granular control. This API requires you to provide each subcomponent, but gives you full control over the structure and layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactNode} from "react"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Popover} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/popover"
import {Portal} from "@qualcomm-ui/react-core/portal"

export function PopoverCompositeDemo(): ReactNode {
  return (
    <Popover.Root>
      <Popover.Anchor>
        <Popover.Trigger>
          <Button emphasis="primary">Show Popover</Button>
        </Popover.Trigger>
      </Popover.Anchor>

      <Portal>
        <Popover.Positioner>
          <Popover.Content>
            <Popover.Arrow />
            <Popover.Label>Label</Popover.Label>
            <Popover.Description>Description</Popover.Description>
          </Popover.Content>
        </Popover.Positioner>
      </Portal>
    </Popover.Root>
  )
}

```

### [Accessibility](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#accessibility)

* The anchor element is automatically associated with the popover:
* When the popover is visible, the anchor element receives the `aria-controls` and `aria-expanded` attributes.
* The anchor element's `aria-haspopup` is always set to `'true'`.
* When the `Popover.Label` component is supplied, the overlay panel's `aria-labelledby` attribute is set to the id of the label.
* When the `Popover.Description` component is supplied, the overlay panel's `aria-describedby` attribute is set to the id of the label.

> \[!note]
> Don't worry about supplying id's to the label, description or popover. If these aren't provided, they'll be generated automatically.

### [API](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#api)

#### [\<Popover>](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#popover)

This section describes the elements of the Popover's composite API.

#### [\<Popover.Label>](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/popover#popover-label)

```json

  {

  },
  {

  },

  }
]
```

## Progress

A visual indicator showing completion progress of tasks and loading states.

```tsx
import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"
```

### [Overview](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#overview)

The progress ring can be in one of two states:

* indeterminate: the default state.
* determinate: assumed when the [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#value) prop is passed.

Indeterminate progress bars keep users informed that work is happening when timeframes are unknown.

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#simple)

The simple API provides a standalone component with built-in layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"

export function ProgressSimpleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-6">
      <Progress className="w-64" label="Indeterminate" />
      <Progress
        hint="Optional hint"
        label="Determinate"
        value={64}
        valueText="64%"
      />
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#composite)

Build with the composite API for granular control. This API requires you to provide each subcomponent, but gives you full control over the structure and layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"

export function ProgressCompositeDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Progress.Root className="w-64" value={64}>
      <Progress.Label>Label</Progress.Label>
      <Progress.Context>
        {(api) => (
          <Progress.ValueText>{`${api.valuePercent}%`}</Progress.ValueText>
        )}
      </Progress.Context>
      <Progress.ValueText />
      <Progress.Track />
      <Progress.Hint>Optional hint</Progress.Hint>
    </Progress.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Label Orientation](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#label-orientation)

Use the [labelOrientation](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#labelOrientation) property to change the positioning of the label and value text relative to the progress bar.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"

export function ProgressLabelOrientationDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-6">
      <Progress
        className="w-96"
        label="Determinate"
        labelOrientation="side"
        value={64}
        valueText="64%"
      />
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Sizes](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#sizes)

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useEffect, useState} from "react"

import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"

export function ProgressSizeDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<number>(10)

  useEffect(() => {
    function increment() {
      setValue((prevValue) => {
        if (prevValue === 100) {
          return 0
        }
        return prevValue + 10
      })
    }
    const timer = setInterval(increment, 1000)
    return () => clearInterval(timer)
  }, [])

  return (
    <div className="flex w-64 flex-col gap-6">
      <Progress label="sm" size="sm" value={value} />
      <Progress label="md" size="md" value={value} />
      <Progress label="lg" size="lg" value={value} />
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Emphasis (Color)](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#emphasis-color)

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"

export function ProgressEmphasisDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <div className="flex w-64 flex-col gap-6">
      <Progress emphasis="primary" />
      <Progress emphasis="neutral" />
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Error Text](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#error-text)

Error messages are displayed using two props:

* [invalid](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#invalid)
* [errorText](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#errorText) (or the `Progress.ErrorText` component when using the composite API)

The error text will only render when `invalid` is true.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Progress} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress"

export function ProgressErrorTextDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Progress
      className="w-64"
      defaultValue={25}
      errorText="Network disconnected, please try again"
      invalid
      label="Loading Module"
      valueText={({valuePercent}) => `${valuePercent}%`}
    />
  )
}

```

### [Shortcuts](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#shortcuts)

#### [Track and Bar](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#track-and-bar)

The `Progress.Track` automatically renders a `Progress.Bar` when no children as passed to it.

```tsx
<Progress.Track />
```

is equivalent to

```tsx
<Progress.Track>
  <Progress.Bar />
</Progress.Track>
```

### [Accessibility](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#accessibility)

* When the value is supplied, the component automatically provides the corresponding `aria-value*` attributes to the element.
* The progress element is automatically marked with `role="progressbar"`.
* The [label](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#label) is automatically associated with the progress bar. If you omit the [label](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#label) property, the `aria-label` of the progress element defaults to the [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#value) when determinate, and "Loading" when indeterminate.

### [API](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#api)

#### [\<Progress>](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#progress)

The `Progress` component supports all props from [Progress.Root](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#progressroot), plus the additional props listed below.

#### [ProgressApi](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress#progress-api)

This API is accessible in JSX via the `<Progress.Context>` component:

```tsx
<Progress.Context>
  {(api: ProgressApi) => {
    return <>{/*...*/}</>
  }}
</Progress.Context>
```

Learn more about this pattern: [Render Props](/render-props)

## Progress Ring

A small visual indicator that communicates ongoing task execution.

```tsx
import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"
```

### [Overview](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#overview)

The progress ring can be in one of two states:

* indeterminate: the default state.
* determinate: assumed when the [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#value) prop is passed.

Indeterminate progress rings keep users informed that work is happening when timeframes are unknown.

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#simple)

For most use cases, you can use the simple `ProgressRing` component.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"

export function ProgressRingSimpleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <ProgressRing />
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#composite)

Build with the composite API for granular control. This API requires you to provide each subcomponent, but gives you full control over the structure and layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"

export function ProgressRingCompositeDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <ProgressRing.Root size="lg">
      <ProgressRing.CircleContainer>
        <ProgressRing.Circle>
          <ProgressRing.Track />
          <ProgressRing.Bar />
        </ProgressRing.Circle>
      </ProgressRing.CircleContainer>

      <ProgressRing.Label>Loading...</ProgressRing.Label>
    </ProgressRing.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#value)

When the [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#value-1) prop is set, the filled portion of the circle will represent the total percent relative to [max](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#max) and [min](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#min). This is referred to as the "determinate" state.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useState} from "react"

import {Minus, Plus} from "lucide-react"

import {IconButton} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"

export function ProgressRingValueDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<number>(0)

  const increment = () => setValue((prevValue) => Math.min(100, prevValue + 10))
  const decrement = () => setValue((prevValue) => Math.max(0, prevValue - 10))

  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-4">
      <ProgressRing
        size="lg"
        value={value}
        valueText={(api) => `${api.valuePercent}%`}
      />
      <div className="flex gap-2">
        <IconButton
          aria-label="Decrement by 10"
          icon={Minus}
          onClick={decrement}
          size="sm"
          variant="outline"
        />
        <IconButton
          aria-label="Increment by 10"
          icon={Plus}
          onClick={increment}
          size="sm"
          variant="outline"
        />
      </div>
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Value Text](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#value-text)

Display the value text by passing a value to the [valueText](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#valuetext) prop. The value text only shows when the size is `lg` or `xl`.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useEffect, useState} from "react"

import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"

export function ProgressRingValueTextDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<number>(0)

  useEffect(() => {
    function increment() {
      setValue((prevValue) => {
        if (prevValue === 100) {
          return 0
        }
        return prevValue + 10
      })
    }
    const timer = setInterval(increment, 1000)
    return () => clearInterval(timer)
  }, [])

  return (
    <ProgressRing
      size="lg"
      value={value}
      valueText={(api) => `${api.valuePercent}%`}
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Sizes](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#sizes)

Use the [size](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#size) prop to change the size of the circle.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useEffect, useState} from "react"

import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"

export function ProgressRingSizesDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<number>(0)

  useEffect(() => {
    function increment() {
      setValue((prevValue) => {
        if (prevValue === 100) {
          return 0
        }
        return prevValue + 10
      })
    }
    const timer = setInterval(increment, 1000)
    return () => clearInterval(timer)
  }, [])

  return (
    <div className="flex items-center gap-4">
      <div className="grid grid-cols-3 items-center justify-items-center gap-x-8 gap-y-4">
        <div className="font-heading-xs text-neutral-primary">xxs</div>
        <ProgressRing size="xxs" value={value} />
        <ProgressRing size="xxs" />

        <div className="font-heading-xs text-neutral-primary">xs</div>
        <ProgressRing size="xs" value={value} />
        <ProgressRing size="xs" />

        <div className="font-heading-xs text-neutral-primary">sm</div>
        <ProgressRing size="sm" value={value} />
        <ProgressRing size="sm" />

        <div className="font-heading-xs text-neutral-primary">md</div>
        <ProgressRing size="md" value={value} />
        <ProgressRing size="md" />

        <div className="font-heading-xs text-neutral-primary">lg</div>
        <ProgressRing
          size="lg"
          value={value}
          valueText={(api) => `${api.valuePercent}%`}
        />
        <ProgressRing size="lg" />

        <div className="font-heading-xs text-neutral-primary">xl</div>
        <ProgressRing
          size="xl"
          value={value}
          valueText={(api) => `${api.valuePercent}%`}
        />
        <ProgressRing size="xl" />
      </div>
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Thickness](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#thickness)

Use the [thickness](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#thickness) prop to change the pixel width of the track and bar.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {ProgressRing} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/progress-ring"

export function ProgressRingThicknessDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <ProgressRing className="py-2" size="lg" thickness={8} />
  )
}

```

### [Shortcuts](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#shortcuts)

The `ProgressRing.Circle` component automatically renders the track and bar components when child. The following two examples are

```tsx
<ProgressRing.Circle />
```

### [Accessibility](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#accessibility)

* When the value is supplied, the component automatically provides the corresponding `aria-value*` attributes to the element.
* The progress element is automatically marked with `role="progressbar"`.
* The [label](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#label) is automatically associated with the progress bar. If you omit the [label](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#label) property, the `aria-label` of the progress element defaults to the [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#value) when determinate, and "Loading" when indeterminate.

### [API](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#api)

#### [\<ProgressRing>](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/progress-ring#progress-ring)

## Radio

A control element that allows the user to choose only one of a predefined set of mutually exclusive options. The singular property of a radio button makes it distinct from checkboxes, where the user can select and unselect any number of items.

```tsx
import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"
```

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#simple)

Basic radio with label using the simple API.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioSimpleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <form>
      <RadioGroup.Root defaultValue="html">
        <RadioGroup.Label>Language</RadioGroup.Label>
        <RadioGroup.Items>
          <Radio label="HTML" value="html" />
          <Radio label="CSS" value="css" />
          <Radio label="TypeScript" value="ts" />
        </RadioGroup.Items>
      </RadioGroup.Root>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#composite)

Build with the composite API for granular control. This API requires you to provide each subcomponent, but gives you full control over the structure and layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioCompositeDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <form>
      <RadioGroup.Root defaultValue="html" name="language">
        <RadioGroup.Label>Language</RadioGroup.Label>
        <RadioGroup.Items>
          <Radio.Root value="html">
            <Radio.Control />
            <Radio.HiddenInput />
            <Radio.Label>HTML</Radio.Label>
          </Radio.Root>
          <Radio.Root value="css">
            <Radio.Control />
            <Radio.HiddenInput />
            <Radio.Label>CSS</Radio.Label>
          </Radio.Root>
          <Radio.Root value="ts">
            <Radio.Control />
            <Radio.HiddenInput />
            <Radio.Label>TypeScript</Radio.Label>
          </Radio.Root>
        </RadioGroup.Items>
      </RadioGroup.Root>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [Layout](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#layout)

Use the composite API when you need to modify the layout of the radio buttons.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioCompositeLayoutDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <form>
      <RadioGroup.Root defaultValue="html" name="language">
        <RadioGroup.Label>Language</RadioGroup.Label>
        <RadioGroup.Items>
          <Radio.Root value="html">
            <Radio.HiddenInput />
            <Radio.Label>HTML</Radio.Label>
            <Radio.Control />
          </Radio.Root>
          <Radio.Root value="css">
            <Radio.HiddenInput />
            <Radio.Label>CSS</Radio.Label>
            <Radio.Control />
          </Radio.Root>
          <Radio.Root value="ts">
            <Radio.HiddenInput />
            <Radio.Label>TypeScript</Radio.Label>
            <Radio.Control />
          </Radio.Root>
        </RadioGroup.Items>
      </RadioGroup.Root>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [Disabled](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#disabled)

When [disabled](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#disabled) is true, the radio group becomes non-interactive and is typically rendered with reduced opacity to indicate its unavailable state.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioDisabledDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <div className="space-y-4">
      <div className="text-neutral-primary font-heading-xxs">Disabled</div>
      <form>
        <RadioGroup.Root defaultValue="html" disabled name="language">
          <RadioGroup.Items>
            <Radio label="HTML" value="html" />
            <Radio label="CSS" value="css" />
            <Radio label="TypeScript" value="ts" />
          </RadioGroup.Items>
        </RadioGroup.Root>
      </form>
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Controlled State](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#controlled-state)

Set the initial value using the [defaultValue](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#defaultValue) prop, or use [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#value) and [onValueChange](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#onValueChange) to control the value manually. These props follow our [controlled state](/patterns/controlled-state) pattern.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useState} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioControlledDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<string | null>("html")

  return (
    <form>
      <RadioGroup.Root
        name="language"
        onValueChange={(nextValue) => {
          console.log("radio value change:", nextValue)
          setValue(nextValue)
        }}
        value={value}
      >
        <RadioGroup.Items>
          <Radio label="HTML" value="html" />
          <Radio label="CSS" value="css" />
          <Radio label="TypeScript" value="ts" />
        </RadioGroup.Items>
      </RadioGroup.Root>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [Orientation](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#orientation)

Controls the layout direction of radio buttons within the group.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioOrientationDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <form>
      <RadioGroup.Root
        defaultValue="html"
        name="language"
        orientation="horizontal"
      >
        <RadioGroup.Label>Language</RadioGroup.Label>
        <RadioGroup.Items>
          <Radio label="HTML" value="html" />
          <Radio label="CSS" value="css" />
          <Radio label="TypeScript" value="ts" />
        </RadioGroup.Items>
      </RadioGroup.Root>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [Sizes](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#sizes)

The radio supports three [sizes](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#size): `sm`, `md` (default), and `lg`.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

export function RadioSizesDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <>
      <RadioGroup.Root defaultValue="html">
        <RadioGroup.Items>
          <Radio label="small (sm)" size="sm" value="sm" />
          <Radio label="medium (md)" size="md" value="md" />
          <Radio label="large (lg)" size="lg" value="lg" />
        </RadioGroup.Items>
      </RadioGroup.Root>
    </>
  )
}

```

### [Forms](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#forms)

Choose the form library that fits your needs—we've built examples with React Hook Form and Tanstack Form to get you started.

#### [React Hook Form](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#react-hook-form)

Use [React Hook Form](https://react-hook-form.com/) to handle the radio state and validation. [ArkType](https://arktype.io/) works great for schema validation if you need it.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {arktypeResolver} from "@hookform/resolvers/arktype"
import {type} from "arktype"
import {Controller, useForm} from "react-hook-form"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

interface FormData {
  framework: string
  language: string
}

const FormSchema = type({
  framework: "string>0",
  language: "string>0",
})

export function RadioReactHookFormDemo(): ReactElement {
  const {control, handleSubmit} = useForm<FormData>({
    defaultValues: {
      framework: "",
      language: "",
    },
    resolver: arktypeResolver(FormSchema),
  })

  return (
    <form
      className="flex w-56 flex-col gap-4"
      onSubmit={(e) => {
        void handleSubmit((data) => console.log(data))(e)
      }}
    >
      <Controller
        control={control}
        name="language"
        render={({
          field: {name, onChange, value, ...fieldProps},
          fieldState: {error},
        }) => {
          return (
            <RadioGroup.Root
              invalid={!!error}
              name={name}
              onValueChange={onChange}
              value={value}
              {...fieldProps}
            >
              <RadioGroup.Label>Programming Language</RadioGroup.Label>
              <RadioGroup.Items>
                <Radio label="JavaScript" value="javascript" />
                <Radio label="TypeScript" value="typescript" />
                <Radio label="Python" value="python" />
              </RadioGroup.Items>
              <RadioGroup.ErrorText>{error?.message}</RadioGroup.ErrorText>
            </RadioGroup.Root>
          )
        }}
      />

      <Controller
        control={control}
        name="framework"
        render={({
          field: {name, onChange, value, ...fieldProps},
          fieldState: {error},
        }) => {
          return (
            <RadioGroup.Root
              invalid={!!error}
              name={name}
              onValueChange={onChange}
              value={value}
              {...fieldProps}
            >
              <RadioGroup.Label>Framework</RadioGroup.Label>
              <RadioGroup.Items>
                <Radio label="React" value="react" />
                <Radio label="Vue" value="vue" />
                <Radio label="Angular" value="angular" />
              </RadioGroup.Items>
              <RadioGroup.ErrorText>{error?.message}</RadioGroup.ErrorText>
            </RadioGroup.Root>
          )
        }}
      />

      <Button
        className="mt-1"
        emphasis="primary"
        size="sm"
        type="submit"
        variant="fill"
      >
        Submit
      </Button>
    </form>
  )
}

```

#### [Tanstack Form](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#tanstack-form)

[Tanstack Form](https://tanstack.com/form/latest/docs/framework/react/guides/basic-concepts) handles radio validation with its built-in validators.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {useForm} from "@tanstack/react-form"

import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Radio, RadioGroup} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/radio"

interface FormData {
  framework: string | null
  language: string | null
}

const defaultFormData: FormData = {
  framework: "",
  language: "",
}

const requiredMessage = "Please select an option"

export function RadioTanstackFormDemo(): ReactElement {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: defaultFormData,
    onSubmit: async ({value}) => {
      console.log(value)
    },
  })

  return (
    <form
      className="flex w-56 flex-col gap-4"
      onSubmit={(event) => {
        event.preventDefault()
        event.stopPropagation()
        void form.handleSubmit()
      }}
    >
      <form.Field
        name="language"
        validators={{
          onChange: (field) => (field.value ? undefined : requiredMessage),
          onSubmit: (field) => (field.value ? undefined : requiredMessage),
        }}
      >
        {({handleChange, name, state}) => {
          const fieldError =
            state.meta.errorMap["onChange"] || state.meta.errorMap["onSubmit"]
          return (
            <RadioGroup.Root
              invalid={!!fieldError}
              name={name}
              onValueChange={handleChange}
              value={state.value}
            >
              <RadioGroup.Label>Programming Language</RadioGroup.Label>
              <RadioGroup.Items>
                <Radio label="JavaScript" value="javascript" />
                <Radio label="TypeScript" value="typescript" />
                <Radio label="Python" value="python" />
              </RadioGroup.Items>
              <RadioGroup.ErrorText>{fieldError}</RadioGroup.ErrorText>
            </RadioGroup.Root>
          )
        }}
      </form.Field>

      <form.Field
        name="framework"
        validators={{
          onChange: (field) => (field.value ? undefined : requiredMessage),
          onSubmit: (field) => (field.value ? undefined : requiredMessage),
        }}
      >
        {({handleChange, name, state}) => {
          const fieldError =
            state.meta.errorMap["onChange"] || state.meta.errorMap["onSubmit"]
          return (
            <RadioGroup.Root
              invalid={!!fieldError}
              name={name}
              onValueChange={handleChange}
              value={state.value}
            >
              <RadioGroup.Label>Framework</RadioGroup.Label>
              <RadioGroup.Items>
                <Radio label="React" value="react" />
                <Radio label="Vue" value="vue" />
                <Radio label="Angular" value="angular" />
              </RadioGroup.Items>
              <RadioGroup.ErrorText>{fieldError}</RadioGroup.ErrorText>
            </RadioGroup.Root>
          )
        }}
      </form.Field>

      <Button
        className="mt-1"
        emphasis="primary"
        size="sm"
        type="submit"
        variant="fill"
      >
        Submit
      </Button>
    </form>
  )
}

```

Tanstack form also supports [ArkType](https://tanstack.com/form/latest/docs/framework/react/guides/validation#validation-through-schema-libraries).

### [Guidelines](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#guidelines)

#### [RadioGroup](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#radio-group)

The Radio is only intended to be used as direct descendants of the `<RadioGroup.Root>` component.

```tsx
/* Won't work alone ❌ */
<Radio label="Label" value="value" />

/* Works as expected ✅ */
<RadioGroup.Root>
  <Radio label="Label" value="value" />
</RadioGroup.Root>
```

#### [Composite Components](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/radio#composite-components)

The Radio's composite components are only intended to be used as direct descendants of the `<Radio.Root>` component.

```tsx
/* Won't work alone ❌ */
<Radio.HiddenInput />
<Radio.Control />

/* Works as expected ✅ */
<Radio.Root>
  <Radio.HiddenInput />
  <Radio.Control />
</Radio.Root>
```

## Segmented Control

```tsx
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"
```

### [Usage](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#usage)

The `SegmentedControl` component allows users to make a selection from multiple options, exclusive (similar to radio buttons) or not (similar to checkboxes).
It is typically used for filtering content, sorting elements or switching between views within the same context.

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#simple)

Use the simple API to create segmented control items using minimal markup.

```tsx
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlSimpleDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root defaultValue={["chart"]}>
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Chart" value="chart" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Table" value="table" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Map" value="map" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#composite)

Build with the composite API for granular control. This API requires you to provide each subcomponent, but gives you full control over the structure and layout.

```tsx
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlCompositeDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root defaultValue={["chart"]}>
      <SegmentedControl.ItemRoot value="chart">
        <SegmentedControl.ItemText>Chart</SegmentedControl.ItemText>
        <SegmentedControl.HiddenInput />
      </SegmentedControl.ItemRoot>
      <SegmentedControl.ItemRoot value="table">
        <SegmentedControl.ItemText>Table</SegmentedControl.ItemText>
        <SegmentedControl.HiddenInput />
      </SegmentedControl.ItemRoot>
      <SegmentedControl.ItemRoot value="map">
        <SegmentedControl.ItemText>Map</SegmentedControl.ItemText>
        <SegmentedControl.HiddenInput />
      </SegmentedControl.ItemRoot>
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Multiple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#multiple)

By default, a segmented control allows for a single selection. Use the [multiple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#multiple-1) prop to create a segmented control that allows for multiple selections.

```tsx
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlMultipleDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root multiple>
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Production" value="prod" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Staging" value="staging" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Development" value="dev" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="QA" value="qa" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Orientation](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#orientation)

Use the [orientation](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#orientation-1) prop to control the layout direction of the `SegmentedControl`. Options include:

* `horizontal`: Items are laid out in a row (default).
* `vertical`: Items are laid out in a column.

```tsx
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlOrientationDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root defaultValue={["chart"]} orientation="vertical">
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Chart" value="chart" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Table" value="table" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Map" value="map" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Icon](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#icon)

Use the [icon](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#icon-1) prop on the `SegmentedControl.Item` or `SegmentedControl.ItemRoot` components to add icons to the segmented control items.

```tsx
import {ChartArea, Map, Table} from "lucide-react"

import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlIconDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root defaultValue={["chart"]}>
      <SegmentedControl.Item icon={ChartArea} text="Chart" value="chart" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item icon={Table} text="Table" value="table" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item icon={Map} text="Map" value="map" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Layout](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#layout)

Use the [layout](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#layout-1) prop to control how the `SegmentedControl` is sized and aligned within its container. Options include:

* `hug`: Content-sized; width matches the items only (default).
* `fill`: Full width; items share space evenly.

```tsx
import type {QdsSegmentedControlLayout} from "@qualcomm-ui/qds-core/segmented-control"
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlLayoutDemo() {
  return (
    <div className="flex w-full flex-col gap-4">
      {(["hug", "fill"] as QdsSegmentedControlLayout[]).map((layout) => (
        <SegmentedControl.Root
          key={layout}
          defaultValue={["chart"]}
          layout={layout}
        >
          <SegmentedControl.Item text="Chart" value="chart" />
          <SegmentedControl.Item text="Table" value="table" />
          <SegmentedControl.Item text="Map" value="map" />
        </SegmentedControl.Root>
      ))}
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Size](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#size)

Use the [size](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#size-1) prop to control the size of the segmented control. The available sizes are `sm`, `md` (default), and `lg`.

```tsx
import type {QdsSegmentedControlSize} from "@qualcomm-ui/qds-core/segmented-control"
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlSizeDemo() {
  return (
    <div className="flex w-full flex-col items-center gap-4">
      {(["sm", "md", "lg"] as QdsSegmentedControlSize[]).map((size) => (
        <SegmentedControl.Root key={size} defaultValue={["chart"]} size={size}>
          <SegmentedControl.Item text="Chart" value="chart" />
          <SegmentedControl.Item text="Table" value="table" />
          <SegmentedControl.Item text="Map" value="map" />
        </SegmentedControl.Root>
      ))}
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Variant](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#variant)

Use the [variant](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#variant-1) prop to control the visual style of the segmented control. The available variants are `primary` (default) and `neutral`.

```tsx
import type {QdsSegmentedControlVariant} from "@qualcomm-ui/qds-core/segmented-control"
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlVariantDemo() {
  return (
    <div className="flex w-full flex-col items-center gap-4">
      {(["primary", "neutral"] as QdsSegmentedControlVariant[]).map(
        (variant) => (
          <SegmentedControl.Root
            key={variant}
            defaultValue={["chart"]}
            variant={variant}
          >
            <SegmentedControl.Item text="Chart" value="chart" />
            <SegmentedControl.Item text="Table" value="table" />
            <SegmentedControl.Item text="Map" value="map" />
          </SegmentedControl.Root>
        ),
      )}
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Disabled](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#disabled)

Use the segmented control's [disabled](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#disabled-1) prop to control the disabled state of the entire control. Use the segmented control item's [disabled](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#disabled-2) prop to control the disabled state of individual items.

```tsx
import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlDisabledDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root disabled>
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Chart" value="chart" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Table" value="table" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Map" value="map" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Controlled state](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#controlled-state)

Use the [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#value-1) prop to control the selected value of the segmented control. This allows for more complex interactions and integration with form libraries.

```tsx
import {useState} from "react"

import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlControlledDemo() {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<string[] | null | undefined>(null)

  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root
      multiple
      onValueChange={(values) => {
        console.log(`Selected values: ${values?.length ? values : "<none>"}`)
        setValue(values)
      }}
      value={value}
    >
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Production" value="prod" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Staging" value="staging" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="Development" value="dev" />
      <SegmentedControl.Item text="QA" value="qa" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

### [Accessibility](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#accessibility)

#### [Keyboard Navigation](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#keyboard-navigation)

Users can navigate the segmented control using the keyboard. The following keys are supported:

* `Tab`/`Shift + Tab`: Move focus between items.
* `Space`/`Enter`: Select the focused item. Unselects an already selected item if [multiple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#multiple-1) is set.

#### [Icon only](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#icon-only)

While it is possible to use icon-only items in the segmented control, it is important to ensure that they are easily understood and accessible.
Pick meaningful icons and use the `aria-label` attribute to describe their purpose.

```tsx
import {ChartArea, Map, Table} from "lucide-react"

import {SegmentedControl} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/segmented-control"

export function SegmentedControlIconOnlyDemo() {
  return (
    <SegmentedControl.Root defaultValue={["chart"]}>
      <SegmentedControl.Item
        aria-label="Chart view"
        icon={ChartArea}
        value="chart"
      />
      <SegmentedControl.Item
        aria-label="Table view"
        icon={Table}
        value="table"
      />
      <SegmentedControl.Item aria-label="Map view" icon={Map} value="map" />
    </SegmentedControl.Root>
  )
}

```

### [API](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/segmented-control#api)

## Select

```tsx
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"
```

### [Overview](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#overview)

* Each select uses the `selectCollection` helper to manage the list of options. This creates a `ListCollection` instance, [documented below](#list-collection).

### [Examples](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#examples)

#### [Simple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#simple)

The simple API provides a standalone component with built-in layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectSimpleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      placeholder="Select a city"
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Composite](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#composite)

Build with the composite API for granular control. This API requires you to provide each subcomponent, but gives you full control over the structure and layout.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"
import {Portal} from "@qualcomm-ui/react-core/portal"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectCompositeDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select.Root
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      placeholder="Select a city"
    >
      <Select.Label>City</Select.Label>

      <Select.Control>
        <Select.ValueText />
        <Select.ClearTrigger />
        <Select.Indicator />
        <Select.ErrorIndicator />
      </Select.Control>

      <Select.HiddenSelect />

      <Portal>
        <Select.Positioner>
          <Select.Content>
            {cityCollection.items.map((item) => {
              const label = cityCollection.stringifyItem(item)
              const value = cityCollection.getItemValue(item)
              return (
                <Select.Item key={value} item={item}>
                  <Select.ItemText>{label}</Select.ItemText>
                  <Select.ItemIndicator />
                </Select.Item>
              )
            })}
          </Select.Content>
        </Select.Positioner>
      </Portal>
    </Select.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [ARIA Label](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#aria-label)

The Select's label is automatically associated with the input element for accessibility. If you omit the label, you should provide an aria-label to the control to give your select an accessible name.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectAriaLabelDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      placeholder="Select a city"
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Content Width](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#content-width)

The [positioning.sameWidth](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#sameWidth) property controls whether the width of the select content matches the width of the trigger element. The default is `true`.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectSameWidthDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      placeholder="Select a city"
      positioning={{sameWidth: false}}
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Trigger Icon](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#trigger-icon)

Use the [icon](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#icon) prop to add an icon to the start of the trigger element.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {MapPin} from "lucide-react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectIconDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      icon={MapPin}
      placeholder="Select a city"
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Items as Objects](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#items-as-objects)

The `items` prop can be an array of objects. Use the [itemLabel](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#itemLabel) and [itemValue](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#itemValue) properties on the [selectCollection](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#ListCollection) to specify the label and value of each item.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  itemLabel: (item) => item.name,
  items: [
    {name: "San Diego", value: "SD"},
    {name: "Nashville", value: "NV"},
    {name: "Denver", value: "DV"},
    {name: "Miami", value: "MI"},
    {name: "Las Vegas", value: "LV"},
    {name: "New York City", value: "NYC"},
    {name: "San Francisco", value: "SF"},
  ],
  itemValue: (item) => item.value,
})

export function SelectItemsDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      placeholder="Select a city"
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Sizes](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#sizes)

This component supports three [size](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#size) options to accommodate different layout densities and design requirements.

The available sizes are `sm`, `md`, and `lg`. The default size is `md`.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: ["San Diego", "Dallas", "Denver"],
})

export function SelectSizesDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col items-center gap-4">
      <Select
        className="w-40"
        collection={cityCollection}
        controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
        placeholder="sm"
        positioning={{sameWidth: true}}
        size="sm"
      />
      <Select
        className="w-48"
        collection={cityCollection}
        controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
        placeholder="md"
        positioning={{sameWidth: true}}
        size="md"
      />
      <Select
        className="w-56"
        collection={cityCollection}
        controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
        placeholder="lg"
        positioning={{sameWidth: true}}
        size="lg"
      />
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Content Height](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#content-height)

Change the height of the item container by adjusting the style of the Content element.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectMaxHeightDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      contentProps={{style: {maxHeight: 240}}}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      placeholder="Select a city"
      positioning={{sameWidth: true}}
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Multiple Selection](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#multiple-selection)

Use the [multiple](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#multiple) prop to allow multiple selections.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectMultipleDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-72"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      multiple
      placeholder="Select a city"
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Controlled State](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#controlled-state)

Set the initial value using the [defaultValue](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#defaultValue) prop, or use [value](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#value) and [onValueChange](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#onValueChange) to control the value manually. These props follow our [controlled state](/patterns/controlled-state) pattern.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useState} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectControlledStateDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<string[]>([cityCollection.firstValue!])

  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      onValueChange={setValue}
      placeholder="Select a city"
      value={value}
    />
  )
}

```

#### [States](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#states)

The following shows how the Select component appears in each interactive state.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectStatesDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <div className="flex w-60 flex-col gap-4">
      <Select
        className="w-48"
        collection={cityCollection}
        defaultValue={["San Diego"]}
        disabled
        label="Disabled"
      />
      <Select
        className="w-48"
        collection={cityCollection}
        defaultValue={["San Diego"]}
        label="Read only"
        readOnly
      />
      <Select
        className="w-48"
        collection={cityCollection}
        defaultValue={["San Diego"]}
        errorText="Invalid"
        invalid
        label="Invalid"
      />
    </div>
  )
}

```

#### [Error Text and Indicator](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#error-text-and-indicator)

Error messages are displayed using two props:

* [invalid](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#invalid)
* [errorText](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#errorText) (or the `Select.ErrorText` component when using the composite API)

The error text and indicator will only render when [invalid](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#invalid) is true.

```tsx
import {type ReactElement, useState} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectErrorDemo(): ReactElement {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<string[]>([])
  return (
    <Select
      className="w-48"
      collection={cityCollection}
      controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
      errorText="You must select a city"
      invalid={!value.length}
      onValueChange={setValue}
      placeholder="Select a city"
      value={value}
    />
  )
}

```

#### [Within Dialog](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#within-dialog)

To use the Select within a Dialog, you need to avoid portalling the `Select.Positioner` to the document's body. To do this using the simple API, set `portalProps.disabled` to `true`:

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Dialog} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/dialog"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectWithinDialogDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Dialog.Root>
      <Dialog.Trigger>
        <Button emphasis="primary" variant="fill">
          Open Dialog
        </Button>
      </Dialog.Trigger>
      <Dialog.FloatingPortal>
        <Dialog.Body>
          <Dialog.Heading>Dialog Title</Dialog.Heading>
          <Dialog.CloseButton />
          <Select
            className="w-48"
            collection={cityCollection}
            controlProps={{"aria-label": "City"}}
            placeholder="Select a city"
            portalProps={{disabled: true}}
          />
        </Dialog.Body>

        <Dialog.Footer>
          <Dialog.CloseTrigger>
            <Button emphasis="primary" size="sm" variant="fill">
              Confirm
            </Button>
          </Dialog.CloseTrigger>
        </Dialog.Footer>
      </Dialog.FloatingPortal>
    </Dialog.Root>
  )
}

```

#### [Within Popover](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#within-popover)

Like with the Dialog, you need to avoid portalling the `Select.Positioner` to the document's body:

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Popover} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/popover"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

export function SelectWithinPopoverDemo(): ReactElement {
  return (
    <Popover
      label="Select Example"
      trigger={<Button emphasis="primary">Show Popover</Button>}
    >
      <Select
        className="w-48"
        collection={cityCollection}
        label="City"
        placeholder="Select a city"
        portalProps={{disabled: true}}
      />
    </Popover>
  )
}

```

### [Forms](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#forms)

Choose the form library that fits your needs—we've built examples with React Hook Form and Tanstack Form to get you started.

#### [React Hook Form](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#react-hook-form)

Use [React Hook Form](https://react-hook-form.com/) to handle the input state and validation. [ArkType](https://arktype.io/) works great for schema validation if you need it.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {type} from "arktype"
import {Controller, type SubmitHandler, useForm} from "react-hook-form"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"
import {createToaster, Toaster} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/toast"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

const valueSchema = type({
  city: type("string[] > 0").configure({
    message: "At least one city must be selected",
  }),
})

type ValueSchema = typeof valueSchema.infer

const toaster = createToaster({
  overlap: true,
  placement: "bottom-end",
})

export function SelectHookFormDemo(): ReactElement {
  const {
    control,
    formState: {isSubmitting},
    handleSubmit,
    setError,
  } = useForm<ValueSchema>({
    defaultValues: {
      city: [],
    },
  })

  const handleFormSubmit: SubmitHandler<ValueSchema> = async (data) => {
    const validation = valueSchema(data)

    if (validation instanceof type.errors) {
      validation.forEach((error) => {
        const field = error.path?.[0] as keyof ValueSchema
        if (field) {
          setError(field, {
            message: error.message,
          })
        }
      })
      return
    }

    toaster.create({
      label: "Form submitted",
      type: "success",
    })
  }

  return (
    <>
      <Toaster toaster={toaster} />
      <form
        className="w-48"
        noValidate
        onSubmit={handleSubmit(handleFormSubmit)}
      >
        <Controller
          control={control}
          name="city"
          render={({field: {onChange, ...fieldProps}, fieldState: {error}}) => (
            <Select
              className="w-full"
              collection={cityCollection}
              errorText={error?.message}
              invalid={!!error}
              label="City"
              onValueChange={(valueStrings) => onChange(valueStrings)}
              placeholder="Select a city"
              required
              {...fieldProps}
            />
          )}
        />
        <div className="mt-2 flex w-full justify-end">
          <Button
            disabled={isSubmitting}
            emphasis="primary"
            type="submit"
            variant="fill"
          >
            Submit
          </Button>
        </div>
      </form>
    </>
  )
}

```

#### [Tanstack Form](https://react-next.qui.qualcomm.com/components/select#tanstack-form)

[Tanstack Form](https://tanstack.com/form/latest/docs/framework/react/guides/basic-concepts) handles validation with its built-in validators.

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from "react"

import {useForm} from "@tanstack/react-form"

import {selectCollection} from "@qualcomm-ui/core/select"
import {Button} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/button"
import {Select} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/select"
import {createToaster, Toaster} from "@qualcomm-ui/react/toast"

const cityCollection = selectCollection({
  items: [
    "San Diego",
    "Nashville",
    "Denver",
    "Miami",
    "Las Vegas",
    "New York City",
    "San Francisco",
  ],
})

const toaster = createToaster({
  overlap: true,
  placement: "bottom-end",
})

export function SelectTanstackFormDemo(): ReactElement {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      city: [] as string[],
    },
    onSubmit: async () => {
      toaster.create({
        label: "Form submitted",
        type: "success",
      })
    },
  })

  return (
    <>
      <Toaster toaster={toaster} />
      <form
        className="w-48"
        noValidate
        onSubmit={(e) => {
          e.preventDefault()
          e.stopPropagation()
          form.handleSubmit()
        }}
      >
        <form.Field
          name="city"
          validators={{
            onChange: ({value}) =>
              value.length === 0
                ? "At least one city must be selected"
                : undefined,
          }}
        >
          {(field) => (
            <Select
              className="w-full"
              collection={cityCollection}
              errorText={field.state.meta.errors[0]}
              invalid={field.state.meta.errors.length > 0}
              label="City"
              onValueChange={field.handleChange}
              placeholder="Select a city"
              required
              value={field.state.value}
            />
          )}
        </form.Field>
        <div className="mt-2 flex w-full justify-end">
          <Button
            disabled={form.state.isSubmitting}
            emphasis="primary"
            type="submit"
            variant="fill"
          >
            Submit
          </Button>
        </div>
      </form>
    </>
  )
}

```

Tanstack form also supports [ArkType](https://tanstack.com/form/latest/docs/framework/react/guides/validation#validation-through-schema-libraries).

```json
  }
]
```
